**Recurrence 2026-07-21 (THR-643) — #184 (`lint:plan-doc` on non-plan reference docs) fired again, identical shape and identical count.** Same trigger as the THR-616 closeout entry above: the Definition of Done *mandates* updating `Docs/plans/2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md` when a change adds or documents a content-facing engine capability, and doing so produces **31 findings across 31 errors** — byte-identical to the THR-616 run — every one of them inapplicable (`Missing ## Engine pillar`, `## Constants table`, `## NFP-compliance table`, `## Coordination block`, `## Done when`, `## Forked-audit verdicts`…). The guide is a standing authoring reference, not a dated `YYYY-MM-DD-topic.md` plan, and has no executor, no coordination block, and no Done-when by design. Advisory, so the commit proceeded. **Three independent tickets have now paid this tax on the same file, with the same count** — the lint fires deterministically whenever the DoD is followed correctly, which means it is training executors to ignore its output wholesale. Durable fix unchanged and still unshipped: scope `lint:plan-doc` to dated plan-doc filenames, or honour a frontmatter opt-out (`lint-plan-doc: false`) for standing references. | ~1 min reading past known-irrelevant output | S | Yes — fires on every DoD-compliant wiring-guide update. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-643 commit.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-644, hourly pickup) — environment (M): browser automation cannot advance the tick loop, because every automated tab reports `document.hidden: true` and the rAF-driven sim is throttled to a standstill.** THR-644's Done-when is a browser criterion ("browser pass @1920×1080 with the economy feed active over 40+ ticks; console shows no duplicate-key warning"), and it is **not reachable from an automated session**. Both surfaces fail the same way: the Browser-pane MCP tab and the Claude-in-Chrome tab each report `document.visibilityState === "hidden"`, so `requestAnimationFrame` is throttled and the tick loop stalls. Clicking Play flips React state but the sim advances exactly **one** tick per click and immediately re-pauses; batching ten clicks nets zero, because an even number of toggles returns to paused. The page itself is healthy — WebGL hex map renders, `window.__DEBUG` is live, 182 console messages with **zero errors and zero duplicate-key warnings at load** — but tick 0–1 is far short of tick 40, where the first settlement demotion actually fires, so the reproduction condition is unreachable by construction. Verified this is not a click-targeting problem: `document.elementFromPoint` at the Play button's centre returns the button itself (`isSame: true`). | ~12 min attempting both browser surfaces before switching strategy. | M | Yes — it will block *any* ticket whose Done-when requires the simulation to run unattended past a handful of ticks. | **Workaround (used here, and strictly better evidence):** assert the invariant headlessly through the CLI, which runs the real `initializeGameState` → `runTick` pipeline. A 169-tick sweep counting per-snapshot duplicate ids over `state.recentEvents` — the exact array `RecentEventsView.tsx:47` renders with `key={evt.id}` — is deterministic, covers every economy trigger rather than whichever happened to fire in a session, and reproduced the precise reported scenario (two same-tick demotions at tick 49 now carrying distinct ids). A browser console read would have sampled one stochastic run; this proves the contract across the whole window. **Suggested fix:** either expose a `window.__DEBUG.tick(n)` bridge so headless sessions can advance the sim deterministically (cheap, and it would unblock the whole class), or amend the DoD so that a "runs for N ticks" criterion is explicitly satisfiable by a CLI sweep, with the browser pass scoped to what it can actually prove — that the surface mounts, renders, and loads clean. As written, the DoD asks for an artifact the environment cannot produce. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-644 verification.

---

## 2026-07-21 — Structurally closed impediment classes (THR-654, Cowork retirement)

The Pure Claude Code Migration deleted the machinery these classes were symptoms of. **Nothing above is rewritten** — these entries remain the historical record of what the friction cost. This section records only that the *cause* is gone, so a recurrence would be a new defect rather than another tally mark. THR-655 confirms the classes stay closed one week after cutover.

- **Skill-sync false positives (~46 recurrences, #169–181, plus the 2026-06-29 gitignored-PNG entry and 62 mentions repo-wide) — CLOSED.** Cause: two skill trees (`.claude/skills/` ↔ `.agents/skills/`) kept in step by `scripts/check-skill-sync.js` and the THR-192 pre-commit hook, which blocked commits over drift in gitignored workspace artifacts that no commit had touched. THR-675 fixed the gitignored-path predicate 2026-07-21; THR-654 removed the duplicate tree, the script, the hook line, and the `check:skill-sync*` npm scripts outright. There is now one tree and nothing to sync. **THR-540 (skip gitignored / `**/outputs/**` paths) closes as moot** — the script it would have fixed no longer exists.
- **Flush false-closes (#162 family) — CLOSED.** Cause: the `plan-pending-commit` label + hourly `flush-plan-docs` task existed because Cowork could not run git, and its auto-flush branches/titles tripped Linear's native close keywords (THR-510/535), which `.github/workflows/flush-close-guard.yml` was built to intercept. A design session now commits its own plan doc via a `docs/plan-*` PR; label, task, skill, and guard workflow are all deleted. The threat vector no longer exists.
- **Mount corruption (#11 working-tree corruption + the 2026-07 `.git/config` truncation) — CLOSED.** Cause: the Cowork VM syncing a mounted copy of the repo. No mount, no sync, no corruption. Note this is distinct from the *harness*-level home-tree parking (THR-671/672/676), which is unrelated to Cowork and remains open.
- **Obsidian-MCP-unreachable family (#66, #71, #75, #86; ~12 occurrences over 8 days) — CLOSED.** Cause: vault writes routed through an MCP that silently dropped `log.md` appends. Vault skills now write the filesystem directly via `OBSIDIAN_VAULT_PATH`, and fail loud if it is unset. The six `.agents/`-only Obsidian skills were retired rather than ported for the same reason.
- **Linear-auth-in-Cowork (#182, #183) — CLOSED.** Cause: the Cowork connector losing Linear authentication, which stranded design sessions that could neither file nor update issues (the migration plan doc itself had to be committed by hand for this reason). CC sessions authenticate through `LINEAR_API_KEY` and ship daily.

**Still open, and unaffected by this change:** `lint:plan-doc` firing on non-plan reference docs (#184, three tickets and counting), `check:wiki-freshness` path-shape false positives, `rg.exe` absence, Linear `save_issue` silent drops (#48) and `orderBy:priority` rejection (#49), and the harness home-tree parking (THR-671/672/676). Retiring Cowork was never going to touch these — they belong to CC's own sandbox and to Linear.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-654, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): two live surfaces were broken by pointers into `.agents/skills/`, and one of them was already broken before the deletion.** Deleting the duplicate skill tree surfaced a class the `check:skill-sync` hook never checked for: **cross-tree pointers**. The hook compared same-named skills for drift; it had nothing to say about a `.claude/` file whose body says "load the `.agents/` version for execution." Two hits. (1) `.claude/skills/attachment-pipeline/SKILL.md` was a **stub delegating to `.agents/skills/attachment-pipeline/`** for "the full orchestrator SKILL.md and all 4 agent prompts" — deleting the tree would have gutted the skill. Investigating found the delegation was false in both directions: the `.agents` copy was a **33-line subset** of the fuller `.claude` file, and the four `agents/*-prompt.md` files it named have **never existed** in the repo at any commit (`git ls-tree -r HEAD` shows only `SKILL.md` under both paths). So the skill has been advertising a 4-pass pipeline with no prompt files behind it for as long as it has existed — a pre-existing defect the deletion merely exposed (filed THR-684). (2) Two `design-council` role prompts listed `.agents/skills/...` context paths for skills that also exist under `.claude/` — a plain path rewrite. | ~8 min; caught by grepping `\.agents/` across live code/config *after* the `git rm`, not before. | M | Yes for the shape — any file-tree deletion needs a reference sweep, and same-name drift checks do not catch body-text pointers. | **Workaround / method that caught it:** after deleting a tree, grep the deleted path across `package.json`, hooks, `scripts/`, `.github/`, and the surviving skill tree — then, for every hit, verify the *target* actually existed rather than assuming the pointer was valid. The second check is what turned hit (1) from a 2-minute path fix into a real finding. **Suggested fix:** none needed structurally — the duplicate tree that made cross-tree pointers expressible is gone. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-654 implementation.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-654, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): `npm run drift-scan` has been crashing on startup, and the weekly Action that consumes it may have been silently dead.** Ran it to confirm a one-line edit to `SKILL_ROOTS` was safe; it never reached the edit. `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for `scripts/lint-rulebook` — the file exists as `scripts/lint-rulebook.ts`, but the import omits the extension, which Node's ESM loader rejects. **Confirmed pre-existing**, not caused by the change: reverted my edit with `git checkout HEAD -- scripts/drift-scan/index.ts`, re-ran, got the byte-identical failure, restored. Filed THR-683. **Why it matters beyond the script:** `.github/workflows/drift-scan.yml` runs this every Friday ~14:00 UTC and posts the four signals as `drift-scan`-labeled Linear issues, and the weekly retrospective reads those issues as its **first input**. If the Action fails the same way, the retro has been running without its primary signal and nobody would see a red X unless they went looking at the Actions tab. | ~4 min including the revert-and-compare. | M | Unknown duration — the failure is deterministic, so it dates to whenever the extensionless import landed. | **Workaround:** none needed for THR-654 (the edit was verified by inspection plus the full test suite). **Suggested fix (THR-683):** add the `.ts` extension at the import site, then check the recent `drift-scan.yml` run history to find out how long the weekly signal has been missing and whether the retro needs a catch-up pass. **Method note worth keeping:** "revert only the file I touched, re-run, compare" is a cheap and conclusive way to separate a pre-existing failure from one you caused — worth reaching for before either chasing or dismissing a red command. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-654 verification.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-654, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): `core.hooksPath` points at the home worktree, so a branch that deletes an npm script cannot satisfy its own pre-commit hook.** `git config core.hooksPath` resolves to `C:\Users\chris\Dev\Projects\TheFantasyWorldSimulator\.husky\_` — the **home** tree — not the session worktree. So every worktree runs the *home* tree's `.husky/pre-commit`, regardless of what its own branch says. This ticket edits that hook (removing the `npm run check:skill-sync` line alongside the script itself), but the home copy is unchanged until the PR merges, so the commit ran the old hook against the new `package.json` and failed with `npm error To see a list of scripts` — a missing-script error that reads nothing like the actual cause. **The general shape:** any branch that removes or renames an npm script referenced by the pre-commit hook is un-committable without `--no-verify`, because the hook it must satisfy is the one on `main`, not the one in its own diff. | ~3 min to diagnose from a misleading npm error. | S | Yes for the shape — rare, but it will recur for any hook-referenced script removal, and the error message actively misdirects. | **Workaround:** run the hook's *surviving* content manually to keep the verify chain honest (here `npm run lint:plan-doc -- --staged`, which passed clean at exit 0), then commit with `--no-verify` and state the specific reason in the commit body rather than leaving a bare bypass. **Suggested fix:** none obviously worth it — this is inherent to sharing one `core.hooksPath` across worktrees, and the alternative (per-worktree hook paths) costs more than it saves. Worth knowing, not worth engineering around. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-654 commit.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-654, `flush-plan-docs` scheduled run) — process-friction (S): the `flush-plan-docs` scheduled task was never actually deleted, only the skill it invokes was.** The "Flush false-closes (#162 family) — CLOSED" note earlier in this file (and CLAUDE.md's scheduled-task registry) both assert that "label, task, skill, and guard workflow are all deleted" as part of THR-654. That is only three-quarters true: `.claude/skills/flush-plan-docs/` is confirmed gone (`Skill` tool returns `Unknown skill: flush-plan-docs`), but `mcp__scheduled-tasks__list_scheduled_tasks` still lists `flush-plan-docs` with `enabled: true`, cron `0 * * * *`, firing hourly at :04 with a `lastRunAt` from earlier this same hour (2026-07-21T09:04). The registration's own `SKILL.md` at `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/flush-plan-docs/SKILL.md` is also still on disk and still instructs invoking the now-deleted skill — so this exact scenario (an hourly session firing, calling `Skill(flush-plan-docs)`, and failing immediately) will keep recurring every hour indefinitely until the registration is removed. | This run: a few minutes of investigation to distinguish "task genuinely has nothing to do" from "task is structurally broken," ending in a no-op rather than any git/Linear write (correctly — deleting the scheduled task itself is outside what this task file authorized). Ongoing: one wasted hourly cycle in perpetuity until fixed. | S | No — deleting/disabling the scheduled task is a `scheduled-tasks` MCP write action this run's instructions did not authorize; flagged via `spawn_task` instead. | **Fix:** run `mcp__scheduled-tasks__delete_scheduled_task({taskId: "flush-plan-docs"})` (or `update_scheduled_task` with `enabled:false` if the registration should be kept for history) in an interactive session where that write is in scope. **Method note:** when a THR ticket's closeout narrative claims "X, Y, Z deleted," verify each artifact independently rather than trusting the aggregate claim — the skill and the scheduler entry are stored and deleted through entirely different mechanisms (`.claude/skills/` git-tracked files vs. the scheduled-tasks MCP's own store), so a `git rm` of one has no effect on the other. | Hourly `flush-plan-docs` scheduled run, 2026-07-21 (post-THR-654).

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-686, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): `esbuild --bundle` silently defeats the `import.meta.url === process.argv[1]` entry-point guard, turning any read-only check that imports a generator into a writer.** `npm run check:process` had been mutating tracked `Docs/authoring-brief.md` mid-run (it broke a `git stash pop`). Both obvious suspects were innocent: `check-authoring-brief.ts` contains no write call at all, and `build-authoring-brief.ts` carried a correct, conventional entry-point guard around its `main()`. The defect is in the *build* step. `check:authoring-brief` runs `esbuild scripts/check-authoring-brief.ts --bundle --outfile=.cache/check-authoring-brief.mjs`, which **inlines the generator module into the check's own entry file**; after bundling, the generator's `import.meta.url` resolves to `.cache/check-authoring-brief.mjs`, which *is* `process.argv[1]`, so the guard evaluates **true** and `main()` runs. Confirmed by grepping the emitted bundle — the guard sits at line 238 of the check's output. **Why it matters beyond this script:** the repo runs ~10 npm scripts through the same `esbuild --bundle` wrapper, so *any* of them that imports a module with a top-level entry-point guard inherits that module's side effect. The guard idiom is load-bearing everywhere else in Node and reads as obviously correct, which is exactly why this survived. | ~15 min: reproduced with a before/after `git status` diff around `npm run check:process`, then bisected the chained scripts and read the bundle. | M | Yes for the shape — the idiom is common and bundling is the repo default for scripts. | **Fix shipped:** gate on the entry file's *basename* (`path.basename(process.argv[1]).startsWith("build-authoring-brief")`) instead of full-path identity — bundling preserves the importer's outfile name, so a bundled check never matches. **Method note worth keeping:** when a "check" mutates state, diff `git status` around the *whole* chain first to confirm, then bisect the chain — reading the scripts alone would never have found this, because every individual script is correct in isolation and the bug lives in how they are compiled together. **Generalizable rule:** a module that exports pure helpers *and* self-executes on import is only safe while nothing bundles it; prefer extracting shared helpers, or gate on something bundling cannot spoof. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-686 item 3.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-686, hourly pickup) — process-friction (S): a ticket's prescribed fix was unworkable against its own named example, and only checking the example caught it.** THR-686 item 2 specified scoping `lint:plan-doc` "to dated `Docs/plans/20??-??-??-*.md` files (or honour a frontmatter opt-out)" so that `systemic-wiring-guide.md` would be exempt. But the wiring guide's real filename is **`2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md`** — it *is* date-prefixed, so the primary suggestion would have left it linted and all 31 false findings intact, while the ticket would have read as satisfied. The two other files it named (`wiring-checklist.md`, `_template.md`) were **already** exempt via existing `PLAN_DOC_SKIP_PATTERNS`, so a filename-pattern approach would have shipped zero behaviour change and closed the ticket. Implemented the parenthetical option (frontmatter opt-out) instead. | ~3 min — cost only because the filename was checked against the proposal before coding. | S | Yes for the shape; ties directly to THR-688 (ticket-authoring rules), which is about tickets encoding snapshots that do not survive contact. | **Method:** before implementing a ticket's prescribed mechanism, run it against the ticket's own named example and confirm it produces the claimed result. A fix that satisfies the ticket's *words* while leaving the ticket's *example* broken is the expensive failure mode, because it closes the issue. **Suggested addition to THR-688:** when a ticket names both a mechanism and an example, the executor's first step is to verify the mechanism actually covers the example. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-686 item 2.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-674, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): `lint:plan-doc` lints `Docs/plans/.intent-proposals/*.md` as if they were plan docs, so salvaging gate artifacts emits a wall of false ERRORs.** Committing 4 recovered intent-proposal files produced ~40 advisory errors (`Missing required section heading: ## Engine pillar`, `## Constants table`, `## Fail-soft table`, `Missing three_pillars metadata block`, …). Intent-proposals are a *different document type* — they are the short action-proposal artifacts the `intent-judge` skill reads (template at `.claude/skills/intent-judge/proposal-template.md`), and they are not supposed to carry three-pillar sections or a constants table. The lint is advisory so nothing was blocked, but the noise buries any genuine finding in the same run, which is the failure mode THR-686 item 2 was filed against for the wiring guide. Note the `.intent-proposals/` directory is *nested under* `Docs/plans/`, so any glob rooted at `Docs/plans/**` picks it up by default. | ~2 min — recognised as noise immediately, but each commit's output had to be scanned to confirm no real finding was hiding in it. | S | Yes — recurs on every commit that touches an intent-proposal, which is every design-session handoff. | **Fix:** add `.intent-proposals/` to `PLAN_DOC_SKIP_PATTERNS` in `scripts/lint-plan-doc.ts` (the same mechanism that already exempts `wiring-checklist.md` and `_template.md`), or give the directory the `> **lint_plan_doc:** exempt` frontmatter marker THR-686 added — the directory-level skip is preferable since it needs doing once rather than per-file. **Method note:** when a lint is scoped by directory glob, check for *nested* directories holding a different document type before trusting the glob. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-674 scope item 1 salvage.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-677, hourly pickup) — stale-reference (M): a "verbatim port" of a recovered prompt would have shipped two dead references, and only checking each claim against live state caught them.** THR-677's `daily-backlog-grooming` source prompt was recovered verbatim (a rare win — the Cowork task delivered its own instructions during a run, and they were pasted into the issue). The obvious move is to copy it across unchanged, since verbatim is precisely what the ticket asked for over guesswork. But two of its instructions were already dead: it opens Step 0 by querying `list_issues state:"In Review"`, and **`In Review` was retired 2026-06-23 by THR-487** (merge = Done) — `list_issue_statuses` confirms the team has no such state, so that query silently returns nothing every single day. Separately, `weekly-project-hygiene`'s on-disk prompt audited `.claude/skills/` vs `.agents/skills/` parity and the THR-192 sync hook, machinery **THR-654 demolished hours earlier on the same day**. Consequence: a faithful port perpetuates checks that cannot fire, and the sweep reports PASS on audits it never actually ran — worse than no check, because it reads as coverage. Workaround: verify every *state name, path, and enforcement mechanism* a ported prompt references against live sources (`list_issue_statuses`, `list_scheduled_tasks`, `ls`) before copying, and record each deliberate divergence in a Provenance section at the foot of the prompt so the next reader can tell a fix from a transcription error. Generalizes beyond prompts: any artifact recovered from a retired system is dated to its retirement, not to today. | Workaround Found? Yes | Session: THR-677 hourly pickup, worktree focused-lamarr-43cc02

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-688, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): THR-686's `lint:plan-doc` date-glob scoping does not exempt *standing* docs that happen to carry a date prefix, so editing the coordination protocol emits 31 false ERRORs.** Committing a 3-file docs change fired the pre-commit hook's `lint:plan-doc --staged` against `Docs/plans/2026-04-13-linear-coordination-protocol.md` and produced 31 findings — `Missing ## Engine pillar`, `## Constants table`, `## Fail-soft table`, `Missing three_pillars metadata block`, and so on. None are real: the file is the **standing coordination protocol**, not a design plan doc, and has no pillars or constants table by design. This is the *third* recorded instance of one class (impediment #184 for `2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md`, the THR-674 entry above for `.intent-proposals/`, this one for the protocol doc), and it is specifically the failure mode THR-686 item 2 was filed to close. It did not close it: the fix scoped the lint to dated `Docs/plans/20??-??-??-*.md`, and every one of these three offenders either *is* date-prefixed or is nested under `Docs/plans/`. **Method note worth keeping:** a non-staged `npm run lint:plan-doc` run showed nothing for this file — only the hook's `--staged` invocation surfaced it, so a pre-commit "clean lint" check that does not mirror the hook's exact arguments proves nothing. I had written "zero findings" into the commit body on the strength of the non-staged run and had to amend it. | ~5 min: scanning 31 findings to confirm none was genuine, then amending a commit message that had recorded a false verification claim. | S | Yes — advisory, non-blocking, so the commit landed; the cost is noise burying real findings and one falsified evidence line. | **Fix:** stop scoping by filename shape — it has now failed three times against three different document types. Use the frontmatter opt-out THR-686 already built (`> **lint_plan_doc:** exempt`) and apply it to the standing docs (`2026-04-13-linear-coordination-protocol.md`, `2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md`), plus a directory-level skip for `.intent-proposals/`. The distinguishing property is "is this a design plan doc?", which is a *declared* property, not one recoverable from the filename. Tracked adjacent to THR-692. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-688.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-689, hourly pickup) — testing (S): an engine test that "runs N ticks" has two invisible ceilings — the doom clock and vitest's 5s default — and the first one silently makes a correct guard-rail assertion false.** Writing the cap regression for `__DEBUG.tick(n)`, the natural assertion for `DEBUG_TICK_MAX = 200` is `expect(result.ticksRun).toBe(200)`. It failed at **185**: on seed 42 / `MAP_SIZE_PRESETS.small` the doom clock ends the run and the state leaves `phase: 'playing'` around tick 185, so the batch correctly stopped early and reported `stoppedReason: 'phase_left_playing'`. The implementation was right and the *test* was wrong — it had coupled a guard-rail assertion to doom-clock pacing. The failure reads like a cap-arithmetic bug, which is the trap: the obvious next move is to "fix" the clamp. Second ceiling, hit immediately after: ~185 real ticks takes **~8s**, over vitest's 5s per-test default, so the corrected test then timed out and needed an explicit `}, 60_000)`. | ~10 min across two red runs before the doom-clock ceiling was recognised as the cause rather than the clamp. | S | Yes | **Workaround:** for any test bounded by a tick count, assert the *guarantee* (`toBeLessThanOrEqual(CAP)`) rather than an exact count, and add an independent witness that the loop is bounded — here, a counting `resolveTargets` callback invoked once per attempted tick, which proves boundedness without depending on how far the sim actually got. Give any test running >~50 real ticks an explicit timeout. **Method note:** when a tick-count assertion fails just under its target, check `state.phase` before suspecting the bound — a run that legitimately ended is indistinguishable from a miscounted loop if you only look at the number. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-689 cap regression test.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-691, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): GitHub's server-side merge ignores `.gitattributes` merge drivers entirely — including the *built-in* `union` — so a `merge=union` attribute cannot keep a PR out of `CONFLICTING`.** THR-691's scope reasoned that GitHub declines *custom* merge drivers (which require a local `git config merge.<name>.driver` GitHub cannot run) but might honour `union`, since it is built into git itself and needs no local configuration. Measured directly rather than assumed, per the handoff's explicit instruction: with `Docs/changelog.md merge=union` committed and live **on the base branch**, two branches appending different rows at the same line produced `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/merges` → **`409 Merge conflict`**, and an equivalent PR read **`mergeable: CONFLICTING`, `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY`** — the precise THR-668 symptom the attribute was meant to prevent. The same branch pair merges **clean (exit 0, both rows, header intact)** locally. Conclusion: GitHub's merge machinery does not consult `.gitattributes` at all; the built-in-vs-custom distinction is irrelevant to it. | The attribute delivers no GitHub-side benefit, so the web "Resolve conflicts" button and auto-merge remain unable to settle closeout-doc conflicts. Had this not been measured, the ticket would have shipped as a silent partial fix and the next idled PR would have gone CONFLICTING anyway, with a plausible-looking `.gitattributes` entry suggesting it should not have. | M | Yes | **Workaround (now documented, not rediscovered per-run):** resolve locally and push — `git fetch origin main && git merge origin/main && git push`. Union then auto-resolves the three closeout docs and the PR leaves CONFLICTING. Written up in `.claude/skills/pull-work/SKILL.md` § "Closeout — resolving a conflicted closeout-docs PR" with the duplicate-row caveat (union keeps *both* sides of every conflicting hunk — right for an appended row, wrong for an edited one, so skim the diff) and the `Docs/project-status.md` exclusion. **Method note worth reusing:** a PR whose base is a scratch branch rather than `main` triggers no CI here (`ci.yml` fires only on `main`), so GitHub-side merge behaviour can be probed empirically for zero Actions minutes. **Second method note:** the first local demo was invalid — a single union merge exiting 0 proves nothing, because git's `ort` strategy frequently merges adjacent insertions cleanly on its own. Any claim that a merge attribute is load-bearing needs the A/B control with the attribute absent. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-691 union-merge verification.

**New 2026-07-21 (encounter-scene-integration design session) — stale-reference (S): the design-audit-pipeline's own inputs have drifted — all three auditors hit fallbacks that worked but cost signal.** Three independent hits in one pipeline run: (1) `Docs/design-brief.md` has no `§ NFPs` section (it carries a different "Six principles" rubric) and its `last_validated_against: 2026-06-11` is 40 days past the 14-day staleness threshold — the NFP auditor fell back to CLAUDE.md per its prompt and tagged `[design-brief-stale]`. (2) `.claude/skills/design-audit-pipeline/agents/vision-prompt.md` points auditors at `Design/Vision/*` — the actual Vision files live at `TheFantasyWorldSimulator/Vision/` (the auditor rediscovered the correct path via design-brief line 64; every future vision audit repeats this detour until the template is fixed). (3) `taste-profile.md` exists at neither path nor anywhere in the repo, so the taste-profile qualitative check is structurally unassessable — the prompt names a file that does not exist. All three fallbacks degraded gracefully (audits completed, verdicts rendered), which is exactly why nobody has noticed: the pipeline reports PASS while quietly auditing against less than it advertises. | ~0 min this session (auditors self-recovered); recurring detour cost on every future design-audit run. | S | Yes — every design-audit-pipeline invocation until fixed. | **Suggested fix (one small ticket):** correct the path in `vision-prompt.md`, either restore/relocate `taste-profile.md` or delete the check from the prompt, and either refresh `design-brief.md`'s NFP section or repoint the NFP prompt at CLAUDE.md permanently. | Encounter-scene-integration design session (Fable), 2026-07-21.

**New 2026-07-22 (THR-692, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): armed auto-merge does not self-heal a `BEHIND` branch under strict branch protection, so an "arm and forget" PR can idle indefinitely with every check green.** Resuming THR-692 found PR #696 open ~10h after the prior run reported "auto-merge armed, CI green" — an accurate report. All required checks *were* `SUCCESS` (`Test · Typecheck · Build`, Vercel), auto-merge *was* enabled, and `mergeable` was `MERGEABLE` with **no conflict**. The blocker was `mergeStateStatus: BEHIND`: `main` had advanced two commits after the PR was armed, and branch protection runs in **strict mode** ("branches must be up to date before merging"), which this repo has had since THR-282. GitHub's auto-merge does *not* update a stale branch on its own unless the repo's "always suggest updating pull request branches" auto-update setting is on — it is not — so the PR sat waiting for a branch update no one was going to perform. This is a **distinct failure mode from impediment #135 and THR-691**, which both cover auto-merge stalling at `DIRTY`/`CONFLICTING` from append-only closeout-doc conflicts and are resolved by a local merge; here there is nothing to resolve, the branch is merely stale. A survey of the board found the class is not isolated: **9 of 15 open PRs carry auto-merge armed and unmerged**, the oldest from 2026-07-03 (#488), i.e. ~19 days of silent idling. | This run: ~5 min to diagnose, ~1 command to clear. Across the lane: unbounded — a PR in this state never merges and never reports failure, so its Linear issue never auto-closes and the next hourly run re-resumes an issue whose work is genuinely complete. | M | Yes | **Workaround:** `gh pr update-branch <N>` — a GitHub-side merge of the base into the head branch, no local tree needed and no worktree checkout required. It re-triggers CI; auto-merge then fires unattended once the required check is green. **Method note worth keeping:** `gh pr view --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus` returns **`UNKNOWN`/`UNKNOWN` on first call** — GitHub computes mergeability lazily and the first query only schedules the computation. A single call is therefore useless for this diagnosis; re-query until it resolves (2–3 calls, a few seconds apart) before concluding anything about a PR's mergeability. Reading the first `UNKNOWN` as "no information available" is what lets a `BEHIND` PR stay invisible. **Systemic fix worth a ticket:** the THR-675 "arm and move on" guidance is sound but incomplete — it needs either the repo's branch auto-update setting enabled (which makes "arm and forget" actually true), or a sweep that reconciles armed-but-unmerged PRs. Filed as THR-702, which also carries the 9-PR backlog for separate triage — most are `docs(plan): batch flush` artifacts from the `flush-plan-docs` task retired in THR-654 and may be obsolete rather than wanted, so they need a per-PR call rather than a bulk merge. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-692 resume.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (THR-696, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): the `BEHIND`-at-arm-time case fires on a *freshly opened* PR, not only on one that idled.** THR-702 documents armed auto-merge stalling at `mergeStateStatus: BEHIND` after a PR sits and `main` advances. This run hit it on a PR that was **seconds old**: the branch was verified at `origin/main` tip immediately before the work started (`git rev-list HEAD..origin/main --count` → 0), but `main` moved during the session, so `gh pr merge --auto --merge` armed straight into `BEHIND`. Cheap containment, already applied here: after arming, read `gh pr view <n> --json autoMergeRequest,mergeStateStatus,mergeable` **once** and run `gh pr update-branch` if the state is `BEHIND` — one extra call, no CI polling, and it moves the PR to `BLOCKED`/`MERGEABLE` (check pending) which auto-merge *can* resolve unattended. This strengthens THR-702's case: the fix belongs in the closeout path unconditionally, not as an idle-PR remedy, because branch-tip freshness at session start says nothing about freshness at arm time.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (THR-696, hourly pickup) — sandbox (S): the DoD's 1920×1080 screenshot remains structurally unobtainable for a game-view surface; two tools each supply half.** The Browser-pane MCP holds a true 1920×1080 viewport and drove the whole verification (DOM assertions, `__DEBUG` calls, shipped-module imports), but `computer{action:"screenshot"}` fails with `Screenshot timed out after 5s: the Browser pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames` — a headless CC session has no displayed pane to composite. Claude-in-Chrome captures successfully but its window is capped by the side panel (1232×969 here, from a 1658×1304 viewport despite `resize_window(1920,1080)`). Workaround used: take the *assertions* at 1920×1080 in the Browser pane and the *image* in Claude-in-Chrome, stating the deviation explicitly in the PR body rather than implying the capture matched the contract. Worth a decision at retro: either the DoD names this split as the sanctioned procedure, or the screenshot clause drops the pixel requirement for game-view surfaces, since no currently available tool satisfies it and each run pays the rediscovery cost.

**New 2026-07-22 (marathon session, THR-698) — tooling (M): `git worktree remove --force` follows a `node_modules` junction and empties the junction TARGET — it wiped the home tree's real `node_modules` mid-session.** A throwaway timing worktree got a `node_modules` junction to the home install (the documented pattern), and `git worktree remove --force` was run with the junction still in place. The recursive delete traversed the reparse point: the home tree's `node_modules` was left with 0 entries, breaking esbuild/vitest/vite for the home tree AND every other worktree junctioned to it (first symptom: the pre-commit hook failing with `'esbuild' is not recognized`, which masqueraded as the known PATH impediment). The junction-cleanup order rule was already written down in session memory and the checked worktree docs — the failure was skipping it for a "trivial" throwaway worktree. | ~12 min: misdiagnosis as the PATH issue, then a full `npm install` in the home tree to restore. Tracked files unaffected (`node_modules` is gitignored). | M | Yes — any session that junctions node_modules. | **Rule:** ALWAYS `cmd /c rmdir "<wt>
ode_modules"` (removes the reparse point only) BEFORE `git worktree remove --force`, for every worktree including throwaways; verify `node_modules\.bin\esbuild` still exists in the home tree after any worktree removal. | Marathon session 2026-07-22, THR-698 timing A/B.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (marathon session, THR-698) — tooling (S): `gh pr view` can report a PR as OPEN/BLOCKED for ~40 minutes after it actually MERGED — the lazy-compute caveat extends to the whole merge-state read, not just the first UNKNOWN.** PR #707 was read as `state: OPEN, mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED, Test·Typecheck·Build IN_PROGRESS` across four separate `gh pr view` calls spanning half an hour — while the Actions API showed the same run's job `completed`/`success` and the PR had in fact merged at 09:22Z. The THR-702 method note ("re-query 2–3 times before concluding") covers the first-UNKNOWN case; this shows stale positives persist far longer. **Workaround:** when a PR seems stuck but its checks look done or timing is anomalous, cross-check the Actions run (`gh api .../actions/runs/<id>` → `status`/`conclusion`) or re-read `state` specifically — `state: MERGED` appeared only on the fifth read. Do not cancel/rerun a CI job on the strength of `gh pr view` alone. | ~10 min of unnecessary waiting + a near-miss on cancelling a green run. | S | Yes | Cross-check via the Actions API before intervening on any "hung" check. | Marathon session 2026-07-22, waiting on #707 for the THR-698 wiring-doc merge.

**New 2026-07-22 (marathon session, THR-703) — tooling (S): the THR-691 local-union-merge remedy silently fails on branches created before the `.gitattributes` union entry landed.** Salvaging PR #641 (branch from 07-20, pre-THR-691 in ancestry terms — its checkout lacked the `merge=union` attributes), `git merge origin/main` CONFLICTED on `Docs/impediments.md` despite the attribute being live on main: git reads merge drivers from the checkout being merged *into*, not from the incoming side. Worse, the first attempt pushed nothing ("Everything up-to-date") because the conflicted merge was never committed, and the worktree was removed with the merge half-done — easy to misread as success. | ~8 min across two attempts. | S | Yes — any pre-2026-07-21 branch needing the closeout-docs merge remedy. | **Workaround:** write `Docs/impediments.md merge=union` (plus the other two union files if needed) into `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/info/attributes` before the merge, remove it after — info/attributes is worktree-common and needs no commit. **Method note:** after any remedy merge, verify a new commit exists and the push moved the ref; a clean-looking exit with "Everything up-to-date" is the failure signature. | Marathon session 2026-07-22, THR-703 #641 salvage.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (marathon session, 2nd same-day) — tooling (L, upgraded): the node_modules junction wipe is not a one-off — the HOURLY LANE reproduces it via pull-work's own closeout.** The home `node_modules` was emptied a second time (~13:00–13:40), with no manual worktree removal in that window. Vector: the fresh-worktree pattern (memory + docs) says to junction `node_modules` from the home install, and pull-work's "Closeout — remove the temporary worktree" section instructed a bare `git worktree remove --force` with **no junction guard** — so any hourly run that followed both instructions wiped the shared install at its own closeout. Two wipes in one day is chronic-class. | ~15 min recovery each (full `npm install`), plus every junctioned session between wipe and reinstall failing with `Cannot find package` errors that masquerade as config/PATH problems. | L | Yes — structurally, on every hourly run until the guard shipped. | **Fix shipped in this entry's PR:** pull-work closeout now mandates `cmd /c rmdir <wt>
ode_modules` (reparse point only; fails harmlessly on real dirs) before `git worktree remove`, plus a post-removal `node_modules/vitest` existence check that stops the session for reinstall if it fails. **Still open (belt-and-braces):** `clean-stale-git.sh` (host reaper, outside the repo) should apply the same rmdir-junction-first guard before reaping any worktree; until then a reaped junctioned worktree can still wipe the install — sessions should prefer not leaving junctioned worktrees idle. | Marathon session 2026-07-22, discovered when THR-711's scoped vitest run failed.

**New 2026-07-22 (marathon continuation) — tooling (S): `__DEBUG.spawnNpc`/`moveAgent` fail with "No location found at that hex" for `{ at: '@hero' }` and `{ at: '<location name>' }` targets in the browser bridge.** The CLI's `spawn npc <role> --at <location>` resolves names/aliases; the browser bridge's option shape either differs or its resolution rejects wilderness-hex anchors, so a reused-binding browser proof (spawn a reusable NPC, then spawn the encounter) was unreachable for THR-713. | ~10 min. | S | Yes — any browser Done-when needing a spawned NPC at a named location. | **Workaround:** prove reuse through the real `resolveSceneCastContext` + `enrichProse` path in a test (gateDutyCastProse pattern); use the CLI for spawn-dependent world setup. | 197 | 1 | 2026-07-22 | tooling | **Volatile timestamp in `ul-dashboard.generated.json` turns queued PRs into silent auto-merge stalls.** The generated UL dashboard carries a per-run timestamp line. `check:generated-freshness` ignores it as volatile, but git does not — so any two open PRs that each ran `npm run prebuild` hold different blobs of the same file. Under strict branch protection, each merge in a queued cascade made the remaining PRs `DIRTY` (a real content conflict, not `BEHIND`); `gh pr update-branch` fails silently on DIRTY, so the armed auto-merges simply never fired and the queue stalled with zero failure signal (the exact silent-stall shape previously seen with BEHIND, but immune to update-branch). | Two PRs (#740, #742) stalled at the end of a 9-PR cascade; ~25 min of manual conflict surgery at the end of a marathon window. | M | Yes | Resolve by regeneration, not by hand: `git checkout --theirs src/data/ul-dashboard.generated.json && npm run generate-ul-dashboard && git add && git commit`. When more queued PRs still carry the file, make the blobs byte-identical across branches (`git checkout <sibling-branch> -- <file>`, commit, push) so the rest of the cascade degrades to plain BEHIND, which watchers handle. **RESOLVED 2026-07-23 (THR-714)** — the durable fix shipped: `generatedAt` is gone from the generator's output, so identical shard sources produce a byte-identical artifact and this file can no longer conflict on a cascade merge. `VOLATILE_JSON_KEYS` in `check-generated-freshness.ts` is now empty, with a note preferring a deterministic generator over registering the next volatile field. A predicate sweep (`npm run prebuild` twice, diffed) confirmed this was the only non-deterministic artifact of the 25 checked. The regeneration workaround above is retained for the historical record and for any future volatile artifact. | M3 economy marathon, 2026-07-22 |

Same substitution shape as #185, but note this is a **DOM** surface — #185's "Browser pane for DOM, Claude-in-Chrome for pixels" split does not rescue it in a headless run with no Chrome connected. **Durable fix:** make the DoD's viewport clause satisfiable by DOM assertion when no capture route can honour the dimensions, rather than nominally requiring an artifact the automation lane cannot produce. | THR-714 hourly pickup, 2026-07-23 |

The repo already contains both conventions (`generate-systems-inventory.ts` extensionless, `drift-scan/index.ts` with `.ts`), chosen implicitly by which runner each script happens to use, so the right convention for a *shared* module is not discoverable by looking at neighbours. **New 2026-07-23 (THR-724, hourly pickup) — diagnosis-quality (M): a plan doc's dormancy diagnosis named the symptom ("content-starved") where the cause was a dead read site, and implementing the plan as written would have shipped nothing.** The THR-724 plan stated break 1 as "generation is content-starved: the only triggers are `completedEncounter.secretDiscovery` and the `secret_discovery` aftermath effect, and zero templates in the live pool author either" — remediation: add the metadata to ~10 live templates. That remediation alone would have produced exactly zero secrets, because the `completedEncounter.secretDiscovery` trigger lives in `phaseEncounterProgressionV2`, which iterates `state.encounterProgress` — a list that is **empty for the entire run** (the live pipeline resolves `state.unifiedActions`). Cheap only because the plan supplied a runtime baseline (0 edges/120 ticks) to disprove against. | M | Yes — this is the general shape of a "dormant system" diagnosis. A symbol grep proves a call site *exists*; it says nothing about whether the call site *executes*. | **Workaround:** none needed — the plan's own guardrail ("if the substrate diverges from the diagnosis, stop and surface") covers it, and the runtime baseline made the divergence provable in one `eval`. **Durable method:** when a diagnosis says "system X is dormant because its triggers are unauthored", verify the trigger's *enclosing collection is non-empty at runtime* before accepting content-starvation as the cause. One `eval state.<collection>.length` separates "nobody wrote the content" from "the reader is dead", and those have completely different remediations. Generalises the existing Debugging Protocol ("verify the noun before the verb") one level up: verify the *loop* before the *trigger*. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-724 implementation.
